ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U553	Looks like your colleagues don't have any questions for you. Thank you! I'm opening negotiations. There are no requests to speak. Is it? Heljo Pikhof wishes to speak on behalf of the Socialists. Here you go!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U554	I realized earlier that Sulev Kannimäe wanted to give a speech, but you took out [his name]. Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Good fellows! In order for the draft under discussion to reach the grand hall of the Riigikogu, the long and thorough work has been preceded, lasting for many years. I am pleased that this has been the result of cooperation with both the Ministry of Defence and the Ministry of Social Affairs and, of course, the union of rescue workers. The draft harmonises, as far as possible, the service regulations of police officers and rescue staff and other special servants and the social guarantees of volunteers in the area of administration of the Ministry of the Interior. One very good consequence of the entry into force of this bill as a law is probably that the draft will encourage volunteering and also ensure equal treatment between volunteers and professionals. At the same time, the creation of crisis assistance for police officers is a step in the right direction. For years, we have been talking about the need to harmonise the level of compensation in the event of the injury or death of volunteers and professional rescuers, both in the event of an attack and in the event of an accident. If, in today's regulation, the compensation for volunteers in this case is ten years' salary and 20 years' salary in the case of professional rescuers, the amendment will result in 20 years' salary in both cases. This amendment concerns relief police, voluntary rescuers, alert center volunteers, voluntary marine rescuers and persons involved in secret cooperation. Social Democrats also consider it important to make favourable changes to the pension for the years earned by the rescuers, as well as to allow for an old-age pension waiting allowance in the past. The draft provides for an increased old-age pension and pension waiting allowance for rescuers beyond 2023 and allows rescuers to receive a earned pension after 2025. Despite the dangerous and health-damaging work of rescuers, they also have a public retirement age, but for health reasons the savior can retire up to three years earlier than the national retirement age would allow. At the same time, it proposes extending the possibility of early retirement to five years for health reasons. That makes perfect sense, given the nature of the work. The draft also allows retired rescue workers to increase their pension according to their retirement status. The collection of earned retirement age will be abolished after 2036. As the Minister said here on the floor, the idea is that people who guarantee internal security must be paid a higher salary while they are working, rather than being [compensated] in the future only by paying their pension. According to similar principles, special pensions are also calculated for prosecutors and military personnel. Now maybe more so... More time, please.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U558	Our sixth item today is the first reading of the draft Act amending the Act on the Accession of the Republic of Austria, the Kingdom of Belgium, the Kingdom of Spain, the Grand Duchy of Luxembourg, the Kingdom of the Netherlands, the French Republic and the Federal Republic of Germany, particularly in the fight against terrorism, cross-border crime and illegal migration to enhance cross-border cooperation with the Treaty. Kristian Jaani, Minister of the Interior of the Speaking Chair, please.
ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U559	Good chairman of the sitting! Honourable Members of the Riigikogu! I would like to introduce you to the draft, the full name of which I will say once, just as the Honorable President did: the draft law amending the Act on Accession to the Treaty to enhance cross-border cooperation between the Republic of Austria, the Kingdom of Belgium, the Kingdom of Spain, the Grand Duchy of Luxembourg, the Kingdom of the Netherlands, the French Republic and the Federal Republic of Germany, in particular in the fight against terrorism, cross-border crime and illegal migration. In short, it is the Prüm bill and I think it says a little more to many. In the background first. The Prüm mechanism aims to improve cross-border cooperation between police and judicial authorities in the Member States of the European Union. By its very nature, it is divided into two parts, offering opportunities for automated data exchange and cross-border police cooperation. Prüm is an exceptionally successful tool in the fight against terrorism and organised crime. This draft does not directly concern either area or have any substantive effect insofar as it is a technical draft. The draft introduces one amendment: the law excludes specifically designated national contact points and subsequently the Government of the Republic has the power to establish them. This will avoid a situation where the law should be amended every time contact points need to be renewed. Following the entry into force of the draft, the Government of the Republic will make three changes to the contact points through its organisation. The Road Administration will be replaced by the Transport Department. Instead of the Information and Analysis Department of the Ministry of Interior, the contact point will be the Police and Border Guard Board, which will also carry out these tasks in practice on a daily basis. Those amendments are necessary as from 1 January 2021 the Maritime Administration, the Air Administration and the Road Administration were merged into the Transport Department and the aforementioned department no longer exists in the Ministry of Internal Affairs since 2012. Thank you for listening, and if necessary, I am ready to answer questions.
ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U560	But there aren't any. Thank you!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U561	Thank you.

Estonia joined this agreement in 2008. Now this law of accession to the Treaty will be slightly amended, as you have just heard from the Minister. As the Minister also mentioned, the proposed amendments are purely technical in nature. Let it be said once again that a good prerequisite for cooperation in the fight against terrorism, cross-border crime and illegal migration is a continuous and rapid exchange of data and effective contacts. This draft amends the existing contact points in the agreement due to changes in the names and structure of the agencies. As such technical changes may continue to occur, the power to establish contact points will be conferred on the Government of the Republic for the future, which is logical. ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U571	Moving to agenda item 7: Draft Law amending the Law on Feedingstuffs, initiated by the Government of the Republic, 499. At first reading, the Minister for Rural Affairs, Urmas Kruuse, will introduce it. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U572	Thank you, Mr President. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! You have before you a draft law amending the Law on Feedingstuffs. The purpose of this law is to bring the feed law into line with the new EU regulation on medicated feed, which replaces the existing directive regulating the production, placing on the market and use of medicated feed. The Medicated Feed Regulation is part of a so-called package of veterinary medicinal products, the joint objective of which is to limit the spread of antimicrobial resistance. The restriction measure is, for example, a ban on the use of medicated feed for prophylactic purposes or to compensate for poor housing conditions. The requirements for medicated feed have not changed significantly compared to the current legislation. At the same time, the scope of the new Medicated Feed Regulation is wider, allowing the production of proper medicated feed for pets in addition to farmed animals. In order not to duplicate the provisions of the directly applicable Regulation, it is necessary to amend or repeal the relevant provisions of the feed law. What is proposed in the draft? Specify the definition of "medicated feed' as feed containing the veterinary medicinal product to be fed to the animal. The term "intermediate product', which is a feed containing a veterinary medicinal product intended for the manufacture of medicated feed, is added. Specify the undertaking's obligation to hold a licence. For example, an undertaking must be authorised to carry out the production and placing on the market of medicated feed or an intermediate product. The undertaking's obligation to provide an economic operator's notification shall also be specified. For example, an undertaking must submit an economic notification if it stores and transports purchased medicated feed intended for its own business or sells medicated feed intended for pets. The requirements for the prescription of medicated feed shall also be laid down. Among other things, the Minister responsible for the sector will be empowered to lay down more detailed requirements, including the content and format of the veterinary prescription, to establish a link with the Waste Act to ensure the proper management of outdated and unused medicated feed or intermediate product as waste. The maximum penalty rates for legal persons will be updated, a new liability provision will be added, before it was in the Pharmaceutical Act, i.e. a provision on non-compliance with the requirements for the prescription of medicated feed. The administrative burden on feed business operators, as envisaged by the draft, is not significantly affected as the submission of an application for authorisation or an economic activity notification is a one-off activity. As the principles for the production of medicated feed remain broadly the same, there is no need for companies already operating to make additional investments. Due to a possible slight increase in the number of operators, changes in the internal organisation and operation of the Agriculture and Food Board may be expected depending on the duties and nature of the officials. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U592	Our eighth item is the first reading of the draft law amending the Tax Administration Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. I'd like a presentation from Secretary of Finance, Keit Pentus-Rosimethus.
ParlaMint-EE_2022-02-09_U593	Thank you, Mr President. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! It's a pleasure to be here again in this beautiful evening's hour. Before you has been prepared for a long time and prepared in cooperation with the sector to amend the tax administration law [the law]. The desire to develop this draft is due to the need to reduce VAT fraud and the payment of envelope wages in the construction sector. This is one of the problems that has been brought to the attention of the Estonian Association of Construction Entrepreneurs for years. For them, the introduction of a single worker card is important, which would increase transparency and help to fix the time spent on the construction site for all workers. According to this draft, reporting will cover larger constructions for which the highest tax risks have been identified. The main contractor's obligation remains to register the work object and rent it if it does not yet exist a suitable registration system. It is the responsibility of all construction companies to register their subcontractors, also to obtain a worker's card for employees and to ensure that they fix entry and exit on the site. In the case of subcontractors, it is the responsibility of the subcontractor to register the presence of persons on the construction site if the period of work is longer than one month and in places where at least 20 persons are employed simultaneously. If this is taken into account in working hours, the reporting in working hours shall cover construction work with a duration of more than 500 working days. The registration system provides daily information on the length of time each person was present on the construction site on the previous day. At least three times a week, this registration system shall transmit data on the time of entry and exit to the construction site in an unpersonal form. It is important that workers' data be collected at the minimum necessary level, which would also mitigate the risk of data distortion. In order to keep the administrative burden at a reasonable level, a large part of the data is pre-filled from, for example, the data from the work register, as well as from the construction register, the business register, the public procurement register, the police and border guards' office and the labour inspectorate's databases. In order to ensure that the thresholds for reporting obligations are and indeed remain optimal, we are planning to review them no later than three years later and then, together with the sector, to assess the impact of the thresholds imposed on the construction sector and to review whether there are grounds for changing the thresholds set and how they are most appropriate. The draft law submitted to you is scheduled to enter into force on October 1, 2023. Of course, the Tax and Customs Board is also constantly working with both the Labour Inspectorate and the Police and Border Guard Board to check the construction sector, but the greatest complexity that the parties have pointed out is precisely the opaque working chain that you are facing with the draft solution. I am pretty sure that all of you in this Chamber now agree that tax fraud or illegal tax advantages do not serve the interests of the State and that is why this proposed amendment of the law is necessary for you. Thank you. I ask you to support this bill.
